<div class='question question'>

How much will $46$ lollipops cost if they cost $\pounds 1.25$ each?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Price of 46 lollipops}    &= 46 \times \text{Price of 1 lollipop} \\\\
                                &= 46 \times 1.25 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 57.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{lollipops}                            &:& \text{price} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \pounds 1.25 \\\\
= & 1 \times 46                                 &:& 1.25 \times 46 \\\\
= & 46                                          &:& \pounds 57.5
\end{array}
$

Price of $46$ lollipops $= \pounds 57.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 57.50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Price of 46 lollipops}    &= 46 \times \text{Price of 1 lollipop} \\\\
                                &= 46 \times 1.25 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 57.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}

= & 1                                           &:& \pounds 1.25 \\\\
= & 1 \times 46                                 &:& 1.25 \times 46 \\\\
= & 46                                          &:& \pounds 57.5
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Price of $46$ lollipops $= \pounds 57.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Jeff has $8$ pizzas to share between himself and his friends.

If each person is to get $\dfrac{2}{3}$ of a pizza how many friends can he feed?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of persons}    &= \dfrac {\text{Total pizzas}} {\text{Pizza per person}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8} { \dfrac {2} {3}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} { 2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{2} \times 3} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
                            &= 12 \\\\
\text{Number of friends}    &= 12 - 1 \\\\
                            &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of persons $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{2}{3} \text{ pizzas} : 1 \text{ people}  &= 8 \text{ pizzas} : a \text{ persons} \\\\
\dfrac{2}{3 \times 1}                           &= \dfrac {8} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} { 2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{2} \times 3} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                               &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of persons}    &= 12 \\\\
\text{Number of friends}    &= 12 - 1 \\\\
                            &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pizzas}                                                           &:& \text{persons} \\\\
= & \dfrac{2}{3}                                                            &:& 1 \\\\
= & \dfrac{2}{3} \times \dfrac{3}{2}                                        &:& 1 \times \dfrac{3}{2} \\\\
= & 1                                                                       &:& \dfrac{3}{2} \\\\
= & 1 \times 8                                                              &:& \dfrac{3 \times 8}{2} \\\\
= & 8                                                                       &:& \dfrac{3 \times 4 \times 2}{2} \\\\
= & 8                                                                       &:& \dfrac{3 \times 4 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
= & 8                                                                       &:& 12
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of persons}    &= 12 \\\\
\text{Number of friends}    &= 12 - 1 \\\\
                            &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of persons}    &= \dfrac {\text{Total pizzas}} {\text{Pizza per person}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8} { \dfrac {2} {3}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} { 2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{2} \times 3} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
                            &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
                            &= 12 \\\\
\text{Number of friends}    &= 12 - 1 \\\\
                            &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

Using equivalent ratio,

<div class='question question'>

One quarter of the pupils in a class are girls. The rest are boys. What is the ratio of girls to boys?

-  $1:1$
-  $1:2$
-  $1:3$ 
-  $1:4$ 
-  $1:5$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

If $\dfrac {1} {4}$ of the pupils are girls, then the boys will be $1 - \dfrac {1} {4} = \dfrac {3} {4}$. Using that, we get

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{girls}                                &:& \text{boys} \\\\
= & \dfrac{1} {4}                               &:& \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4} {4} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& 3
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$1:3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>If $\dfrac {1} {4}$ of the pupils are girls, then the boys will be $1 - \dfrac {1} {4} = \dfrac {3} {4}$. Using that, we get
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{girls}                                &:& \text{boys} \\\\
= & \dfrac{1} {4}                               &:& \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4} {4} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& 3
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1:3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On a train there are $140$ men and $200$ women. What is the ratio of men to women written in its simplest form?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Ratio}      &= \dfrac {\text{Number of men}} {\text{Number of women}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {140} {200} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {20 \times 7} {20 \times 10} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 7} {\cancel {20} \times 10} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {7} {10} \\\\
                  &= 7 : 10
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7 : 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                  &= \dfrac {140} {200} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {20 \times 7} {20 \times 10} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 7} {\cancel {20} \times 10} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {7} {10} \\\\
                  &= 7 : 10
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let length of the model $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
50 \ \text{m} : 1 \ \text{m}                    &= 120 \ \text{m} : a \ \text{m} \\\\
\dfrac {50} {1}                                 &= \dfrac {120} {a} \\\\
50 \times a                                     &= 120 \times 1 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {120} {50} \ \text{m} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {120 \times 100} {50} \ \text{cm} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {120 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {120 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 240 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the model $= 240 \ \text{cm}$

</div>

$

= & 50 \ \text{m}                               &:& 1 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
= & 50                                          &:& 100 \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {100} {50} \\\\
= & 1 \times 120                                &:& \dfrac {100 \times 120} {50} \\\\
= & 120                                         &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2 \times 120} {50} \\\\
= & 120                                         &:& \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 2 \times 120} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 120                                         &:& 2 \times 120 \\\\
= & 120 \ \text{m}                              &:& 240 \ \text{cm}
\end{array}
$

Length of the model $= 240 \ \text{cm}$

</div>

My friend Sanjay is making a scale model of the London Ear (a famous landmark in London). 
The London Ear is $80 \ \text{m}$ tall and his model is $150 \ \text{cm}$ tall. What is the scale of the model, in its simplest form?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Scale}      &= \dfrac {\text{Length of the model}} {\text{Length of the tower}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {150 \ \text{cm}} {80 \ \text{m}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {150 \ \text{cm}} {80 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 3} {80 \times 50 \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 3} {80 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {3} {80 \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {3} {160} \\\\
                  &= 3 : 160
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 : 160$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                  &= \dfrac {150 \ \text{cm}} {80 \ \text{m}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {150 \ \text{cm}} {80 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 3} {80 \times 50 \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 3} {80 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {3} {80 \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {3} {160} \\\\
                  &= 3 : 160
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let length of the model yacht $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{m} : 30 \ \text{m}                    &= a \ \text{m} : 9 \ \text{m} \\\\
\dfrac{1} {30}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {9} \\\\
a \times 30                                     &= 9 \times 1 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9} {30} \ \text{m} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \ \text{cm} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 3 \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the model yacht $= 30 \ \text{cm}$

= & 30 \ \text{m}                 &:& 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {100} {30} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                    &:& \dfrac {100 \times 9} {30} \\\\
= & 9                             &:& \dfrac {100 \times 3 \times 3} {30} \\\\
= & 9                             &:& \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \times 3 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \cancel 0} \\\\
= & 9                             &:& 10 \times 3 \\\\
= & 9 \ \text{m}                  &:& 30 \ \text{cm}
\end{array}
$

Length of the model yacht $= 30 \ \text{cm}$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

My friend Dave is making a scale model of Tower Bridge. Tower Bridge is $60 \ \text{m}$ tall and his model is $120 \ \text{cm}$ 
tall. What is the scale of the model, in its simplest form?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Scale}      &= \dfrac {\text{Model}} {\text{Tower}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {120 \ \text{cm}} {60 \ \text{m}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {120 \ \text{cm}} {60 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {60 \times 2 \times 50} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {60}} {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 2 \times 50} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {1} {50} \\\\
                  &= 1 : 50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 : 50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Scale}      &= \dfrac {\text{Model}} {\text{Tower}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {120 \ \text{cm}} {60 \ \text{m}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {120 \ \text{cm}} {60 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {60 \times 2 \times 50} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {60}} {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 2 \times 50} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {1} {50} \\\\
                  &= 1 : 50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A litre of water wieghs $1 \ \text{kg}$ and a litre of ice weighs $900 \ \text{g}$.

How many more grams will $6 \ \text{litres}$ of water weigh compared to $5 \ \text{litres}$ of ice?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Weight of 6 litres of water}    &= 6 \times 1 \\\\
                                      &= 6 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                      &= 6 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                      &= 6000 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Weight of 5 litres of ice}      &= 5 \times 900 \\\\
                                      &= 4500 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Difference}                     &= 6000 - 4500 \\\\
                                      &= 1500 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Weight of 6 litres of water}    &= 6 \times 1 \\\\
                                      &= 6 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                      &= 6 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                      &= 6000 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Weight of 5 litres of ice}      &= 5 \times 900 \\\\
                                      &= 4500 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Difference}                     &= 6000 - 4500 \\\\
                                      &= 1500 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$600 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>22706088-179E-4797-A8FB-6900DD74BDC2</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-4:1:27</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$5$ toffees weigh the same as $6$ maltesers and $3$ toffees.

How many maltesers would weigh the same as $9$ toffees?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of maltesers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
(5 - 3) \ \text{toffees} : 6 \ \text{maltesers} &= 9 \ \text{toffees} : a \ \text{maltesers} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {6}                                   &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
2a                                              &= 9 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

Number of maltesers $= 27$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{toffees}                    &:& \text{maltesers} \\\\
= & (5 - 3)                           &:& 6 \\\\
= & 2                                 &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                                 &:& \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                        &:& \dfrac {6 \times 9} {2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 9} {2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 9} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& 3 \times 9 \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& 27
\end{array}
$

Number of maltesers $= 27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of maltesers $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(5 - 3) \ \text{toffees} : 6 \ \text{maltesers} &= 9 \ \text{toffees} : a \ \text{maltesers} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {6}                                   &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
2a                                              &= 9 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of maltesers $= 27$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{toffees}                    &:& \text{maltesers} \\\\
= & (5 - 3)                           &:& 6 \\\\
= & 2                                 &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                                 &:& \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                        &:& \dfrac {6 \times 9} {2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 9} {2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 9} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& 3 \times 9 \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& 27
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of maltesers $= 27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

My flask holds $480 \ \text{ml}$ when it is one quarter empty. 

How much does it hold when it is one quarter full?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let quantity of water it hold at one quarter full $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\left( 1 - \dfrac {1} {4} \right) \ \text{fraction} : 480 \ \text{volume}  &= \dfrac {1} {4} \ \text{fraction} : a \ \text{volume} \\\\
\dfrac{\left( 1 - \dfrac {1} {4} \right)} {480}                            &= \dfrac {\dfrac {1} {4}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{\dfrac {3} {4}} {480}                                               &= \dfrac {\dfrac {1} {4}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {4 \times 480}                                                  &= \dfrac {1} {4 \times a} \\\\
3 \times 4 \times a                                                        &= 4 \times 480 \\\\
a                                                                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 480} {3 \times 4} \\\\
a                                                                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 160} {3 \times 4} \\\\
a                                                                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times 160} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                                                          &= 160 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Quantity of water it hold at one quarter full $= 160 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fraction}                                   &:& \text{volume} \\\\
= & \left( 1 - \dfrac {1} {4} \right)                 &:& 480 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & \left( \dfrac {4} {4} - \dfrac {1} {4} \right)    &:& 480 \\\\
= & \dfrac {4 - 1} {4}                                &:& 480 \\\\
= & \dfrac {3} {4}                                    &:& 480 \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {4}                                    &:& \dfrac {480} {3} \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {4}                                    &:& \dfrac {3 \times 160} {3} \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {4}                                    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 160} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {4}                                    &:& 160 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Quantity of water it hold at one quarter full $= 160 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$160 \ \text{ml}$

</div>

\dfrac{\left( 1 - \dfrac {1} {4} \right)} {480}                            &= \dfrac {\dfrac {1} {4}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{\dfrac {3} {4}} {480}                                               &= \dfrac {\dfrac {1} {4}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {4 \times 480}                                                  &= \dfrac {1} {4 \times a} \\\\
3 \times 4 \times a                                                        &= 4 \times 480 \\\\
a                                                                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 480} {3 \times 4} \\\\
a                                                                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 160} {3 \times 4} \\\\
a                                                                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times 160} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                                                          &= 160 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Quantity of water it hold at one quarter full $= 160 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>

In the diagram, the small squares are all the same size. 

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

What fraction of the large square is shaded?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's count the shaded and unshaded squares.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Shaded squares}       &= 9 \\\\
\text{Unshaded squares}     &= 4 + 8 \times \dfrac {1} {2} + 4 \times \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
                            &= 4 + \dfrac {8} {4} + \dfrac {4} {4} \\\\
                            &= 4 + 2 + 1 \\\\
                            &= 7 \\\\
\text{Fraction shaded}      &= \dfrac {9} {9 + 7} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {9} {16}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {9} {16}$

</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2020--sample-paper-6:1:27</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$5$ toffees weigh the same as $6$ maltesers and $3$ toffees.

How many maltesers would weigh the same as $9$ toffees?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of maltesers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
(5 - 3) \ \text{toffees} : 6 \ \text{maltesers} &= 9 \ \text{toffees} : a \ \text{maltesers} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {6}                                   &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
2a                                              &= 9 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

Number of maltesers $= 27$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{toffees}                    &:& \text{maltesers} \\\\
= & (5 - 3)                           &:& 6 \\\\
= & 2                                 &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                                 &:& \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                        &:& \dfrac {6 \times 9} {2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 9} {2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 9} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& 3 \times 9 \\\\
= & 9                                 &:& 27
\end{array}
$

Number of maltesers $= 27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of maltesers $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(5 - 3) \ \text{toffees} : 6 \ \text{maltesers} &= 9 \ \text{toffees} : a \ \text{maltesers} \\\\

2a                                              &= 9 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of maltesers $= 27$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much **water** does she need to make **eight** glasses of squash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount of water required}       &= 8 \times 200 - 320 \\\\
                                      &= 1600 - 320 \\\\
                                      &= 1280 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1280 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much **cordial** does she need to make **three** glasses of squash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let quantity of cordial needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
320 \ \text{ml} : 8 \ \text{glasses}            &= a \ \text{ml} : 3 \ \text{glasses} \\\\
\dfrac{320} {8}                                 &= \dfrac {a} {3} \\\\
8 \times a                                      &= 320 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {320 \times 3} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 40 \times 3} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 40 \times 3} {\cancel 8} \\\\
a                                               &= 40 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Cordial needed $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{glasses}                          &:& \text{cordial} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 320 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {320} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 3                              &:& \dfrac {320 \times 3} {8} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& \dfrac {8 \times 40 \times 3} {8} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 40 \times 3} {\cancel 8} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 40 \times 3 \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Cordial needed $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}

                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 23} {\cancel 7} \\\\
                            &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='answers'>

$23$

<div class='papername'>
<p>christs-hospital-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1:2:4</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='question question'>

**Boys and girls**

At Casterbridge Academy the ratio of boys to girls is exactly $5{:}6$.

There are $200$ **boys** at Casterbridge Academy.

Work out the number of **girls** at Casterbridge Academy.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of girsl $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{boys} : 6 \ \text{girls}              &= 200 \ \text{boys} : a \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac{5} {6}                                   &= \dfrac {200} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 200 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {200 \times 6} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 40 \times 6} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 40 \times 6} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 40 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 240
\end{aligned}
$

Number of girls $= 240$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{boys}                   &:& \text{girls} \\\\
= & 5                             &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{6} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 200                  &:& \dfrac{6 \times 200} {5} \\\\
= & 200                           &:& \dfrac{6 \times 5 \times 40} {5} \\\\
= & 200                           &:& \dfrac{6 \times \cancel 5 \times 40} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 200                           &:& 6 \times 40 \\\\
= & 200                           &:& 240
\end{array}
$

Number of girls $= 240$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of girsl $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{boys} : 6 \ \text{girls}              &= 200 \ \text{boys} : a \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac{5} {6}                                   &= \dfrac {200} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 200 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {200 \times 6} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 40 \times 6} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 40 \times 6} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 40 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 240
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of girls $= 240$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{boys}                   &:& \text{girls} \\\\
= & 5                             &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{6} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 200                  &:& \dfrac{6 \times 200} {5} \\\\
= & 200                           &:& \dfrac{6 \times 5 \times 40} {5} \\\\
= & 200                           &:& \dfrac{6 \times \cancel 5 \times 40} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 200                           &:& 6 \times 40 \\\\
= & 200                           &:& 240
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of girls $= 240$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$240$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

There are $10 \ \text{millimetres}$ in a centimetre and $100 \ \text{centimetres}$ in a metre.

How many millimetres are there in $25.1 \ \text{metres}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 25.1 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 25.1 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 25.1 \times 100 \times 10 \ \text{mm} \\\\
&= 25100 \ \text{mm}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25100 \ \text{mm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
&= 25.1 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 25.1 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 25.1 \times 100 \times 10 \ \text{mm} \\\\
&= 25100 \ \text{mm}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

$5 \ \text{gallons} = 22.82 \ \text{litres}$

Which is nearest to $1 \ \text{gallon}$?

$4.528 \quad 4.448 \quad 4.645 \quad 4.565 \quad 4.504$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio, 

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{gallons}      &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 5                   &:& 22.82 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82} {5} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {45.64} {10} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{gallon}   &:& 4.564 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

It is nearest to $4.565$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.565$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio, 
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{gallons}      &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 5                   &:& 22.82 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82} {5} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {45.64} {10} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{gallon}   &:& 4.564 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
It is nearest to $4.565$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4.565$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$48$ pencils cost $\pounds 11.04$.  What is the cost of each pencil?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pencil}       &= \pounds \dfrac {11.04} {48} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {1104} {48} \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {23 \times 48} {48} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {23 \times \cancel {48}} {\cancel {48}} \\\\
                              &= 23 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pencils}                &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 48                            &:& \pounds 11.04 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \pounds \dfrac {11.04} {48} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {1104} {48} \text{p} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {48 \times 23} {48} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {\cancel {48} \times 23} {\cancel {48}} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& 23 \text{p}
\end{array}
$

Cost of each pencil $= 0.23 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$23 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pencil}       &= \pounds \dfrac {11.04} {48} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {1104} {48} \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {23 \times 48} {48} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {23 \times \cancel {48}} {\cancel {48}} \\\\
                              &= 23 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pencils}                &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 48                            &:& \pounds 11.04 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \pounds \dfrac {11.04} {48} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {1104} {48} \text{p} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {48 \times 23} {48} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac {\cancel {48} \times 23} {\cancel {48}} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& 23 \text{p}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of each pencil $= 0.23 \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

How many chocolate bars are in $16$ boxes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Chocolate bars}       &= 24 \times 16 \\\\
                            &= 384
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{boxes}                  &:& \text{chocolate bars} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& 24 \\\\
= & 1 \times 16                   &:& 24 \times 16 \\\\
= & 16                            &:& 384
\end{array}
$

Chocolate bars $= 384$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$384$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

= & 1                             &:& 24 \\\\
= & 1 \times 16                   &:& 24 \times 16 \\\\
= & 16                            &:& 384
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Chocolate bars $= 384$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

= & 5                   &:& 22.82 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82} {5} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {45.64} {10} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{gallon}   &:& 4.564 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

It is nearest to $4.565$.

<div class='question question'>

$36$ pencils cost $\pounds 7.92$.  What is the cost of each pencil?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pencil}       &= \dfrac {\pounds 7.92} {36} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {792} {36} \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {36 \times 22} {36} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {36} \times 22} {\cancel {36}} \\\\
                              &= 22 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pencils}                &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 36                            &:& \pounds 7.92 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{\pounds 7.92} {36} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{792} {36} \text{p} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{36 \times 22} {36} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{\cancel {36} \times 22} {\cancel {36}} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& 22 \text{p}
\end{array}
$

Cost of pencil $= 22 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$22 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                              &= 22 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

There is $5.5 \ \text{g}$ of sugar in every $10 \ \text{g}$ of jam. How much sugar is in $600 \ \text{g}$ of jam?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of sugar $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5.5 \ \text{g} : 10 \ \text{g}                  &= a \ \text{g} : 600 \ \text{g} \\\\
\dfrac{5.5} {10}                                &= \dfrac {a} {600} \\\\
a \times 10                                     &=  5.5 \times 600 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 600} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 10 \times 60} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 1 \cancel {0} \times 60} {1 \cancel {0}} \\\\
a                                               &= 5.5 \times 60 \\\\
a                                               &= 330 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{jam}                    &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 10 \ \text{g}                 &:& 5.5 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 10 \times 60                  &:& 5.5 \times 60 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{g}                &:& 330 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Amount of sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$330 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of sugar $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5.5 \ \text{g} : 10 \ \text{g}                  &= a \ \text{g} : 600 \ \text{g} \\\\
\dfrac{5.5} {10}                                &= \dfrac {a} {600} \\\\
a \times 10                                     &=  5.5 \times 600 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 600} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 10 \times 60} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 1 \cancel {0} \times 60} {1 \cancel {0}} \\\\
a                                               &= 5.5 \times 60 \\\\
a                                               &= 330 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{jam}                    &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 10 \ \text{g}                 &:& 5.5 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 10 \times 60                  &:& 5.5 \times 60 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{g}                &:& 330 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

$5 \ \text{gallons} = 22.82 \ \text{litres}$

Which is nearest to $1 \ \text{gallon}$?

$4.528 \quad  4.448 \quad   4.645 \quad  4.565 \quad  4.504$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{gallons}      &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 5                   &:& 22.82 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82} {5} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {22.82 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {45.64} {10} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& 4.564
\end{array}
$

It is nearest to $4.565$.

</div>
<div class='working'>

$

\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$36$ pencils cost $\pounds 7.92$. What is the cost of each pencil?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pencil}       &= \pounds \dfrac {7.92} {36} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {792} {36} \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {36 \times 22} {36} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {36} \times 22} {\cancel {36}} \\\\
                              &= 22 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pencils}                &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 36                            &:& \pounds 7.92 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \pounds \dfrac{7.92} {36} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{792} {36} \text{p} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{36 \times 22} {36} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{\cancel {36} \times 22} {\cancel {36}} \\\\
= & 1                             &:& 22 \text{p}
\end{array}
$

Cost of each pencil $= 22 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$22 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                              &= 22 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

One pint is $568\ \text{ml}$. How many $\text{ml}$ is half a pint?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Half pint}            &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 568 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {568} {2}  \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 284} {2}  \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 284} {\cancel 2}  \\\\
                            &= 284 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let half mint $= a \ \text{ml}$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{pint} : 568 \ \text{ml}               &= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{pint} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {568}                                &= \dfrac {\dfrac {1} {2}} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times {568} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {568} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 284} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 284} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 284 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Half pint $= 284 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pint}                     &:& \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                               &:& 568 \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {568} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {2 \times 284} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 284} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{pint}    &:& 284 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Half pint $= 284 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$284 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                            &= \dfrac {568} {2}  \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 284} {2}  \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 284} {\cancel 2}  \\\\
                            &= 284 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Gym membership costs $\pounds 16$ a month. How much will it cost for a year?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost for 1 year membership}      &= 12 \times 16 \\\\
                                       &= \pounds 192
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{duration}                    &:& \text{price} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{month}                   &:& \pounds 16 \\\\
= & 1 \times 12 \ \text{months}        &:& 16 \times 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{year}                    &:& \pounds 192
\end{array}
$

Cost of $1$ year Gym membership $= \pounds 192$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 192$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost for 1 year membership}      &= 12 \times 16 \\\\
                                       &= \pounds 192
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{duration}                    &:& \text{price} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{month}                   &:& \pounds 16 \\\\
= & 1 \times 12 \ \text{months}        &:& 16 \times 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{year}                    &:& \pounds 192
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of $1$ year Gym membership $= \pounds 192$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 192$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A prime number has exactly $2$ factors: itself and $1$.

The numbers from $1$ to $20$ inclusive are written on $20$ cards. Write down the **proportion** of all the cards that have a 
prime number written on them, giving your answer as a decimal.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Prime numbers up to 20} &= 2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19 \\\\
\text{Count}                  &= 8 \\\\
\text {Proportion}            &= \dfrac {8} {20} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {8 \times 5} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40} {100} \\\\
                              &= 0.4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Prime numbers up to 20} &= 2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19 \\\\
\text{Count}                  &= 8 \\\\
\text {Proportion}            &= \dfrac {8} {20} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {8 \times 5} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40} {100} \\\\
                              &= 0.4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$0.4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out how long it would take for $2$ chimps to eat $6$ bananas.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of bananas}                    &:& \text{time taken to eat bananas} \\\\
= & 3                                           &:& 4 \ \text{min} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& \dfrac {4 \times 6} {3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 2} {3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 3 \times 2} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& 4 \times 2 \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& 8 \ \text{min} \\\\
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of bananas}                    &:& \text{time taken to eat bananas} \\\\
= & 3                                           &:& 4 \ \text{min} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& \dfrac {4 \times 6} {3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 2} {3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 3 \times 2} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& 4 \times 2 \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& 8 \ \text{min} \\\\
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out how many chimps would be required to eat $21$ bananas in $8 \ \text{minutes}$. 
(You can assume that the tree has a large number of bananas available and that the chimps all have big appetites!)

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The same monkey will eat 6 bananas in 8 minutes} \\\\
\text{Which means 1 monkey will eat 3 bananas in 8 minutes} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 21} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 7 \times 3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 7 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 7
\text{Hence, we need 7 monkey to eat 21 bananas in 8 minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The same monkey will eat 6 bananas in 8 minutes} \\\\
\text{Which means 1 monkey will eat 3 bananas in 8 minutes} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 21} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 7 \times 3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 7 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 7
\text{Hence, we need 7 monkey to eat 21 bananas in 8 minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

At the school medical centre, there are enough plasters to patch up $12$ cut knees per day for $30 \ \text{days}$. 
Work out how long the plasters would last if the medical centre were having to treat $20$ boys with cut knees a day.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total plasters}     &= 12 \times 30 \\\\
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 30} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 3 \times 10} {2 \times 10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac { \cancel{2} \times 6 \times 3 \times 1 \cancel{0}} { \cancel{2} \times 1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                          &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18 \ \text{days}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total plasters}     &= 12 \times 30 \\\\
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 30} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 3 \times 10} {2 \times 10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac { \cancel{2} \times 6 \times 3 \times 1 \cancel{0}} { \cancel{2} \times 1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                          &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18 \ \text{days}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mr Rutter buys a length of material costing $\pounds 25.50$.
How many metres does he buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Length}                                 &= \dfrac {25.5} {10.2} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {255} {102} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 51} {2 \times 51} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {51}} {2 \times \cancel {51}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {5} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 2.5 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.5 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Length}                                 &= \dfrac {25.5} {10.2} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {255} {102} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 51} {2 \times 51} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {51}} {2 \times \cancel {51}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {5} {2} \\\\
                                              &= 2.5 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In $1970$, $\pounds 1$ was worth the same as $\text{\textdollar} 2.50$.
How many pounds was $\text{\textdollar} 1000$ worth? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value (in $\pounds$) $= p$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 : 2.50                &= p : 1000 \\\\
\dfrac{1}{2.5}          &= \dfrac {p} {1000} \\\\
p                       &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1} {2.5} \\\\
p                       &= \dfrac {25 \times 400 \times 10} {25} \\\\
p                       &= \dfrac {\cancel{25} \times 40 \times 10} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
p                       &= \pounds 400
\end{aligned}
$

Value $= \pounds 400$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                 &:& \text{\textdollar} \\\\
= & 1                       &:& 2.50 \\\\
= & 1 \times 400            &:& 2.50 \times 400 \\\\
= & \pounds 400             &:& \text{\textdollar} 1000
\end{array}
$

Value $= \pounds 400$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 400$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value (in $\pounds$) $= p$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1 : 2.50                &= p : 1000 \\\\
\dfrac{1}{2.5}          &= \dfrac {p} {1000} \\\\
p                       &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1} {2.5} \\\\
p                       &= \dfrac {25 \times 400 \times 10} {25} \\\\
p                       &= \dfrac {\cancel{25} \times 40 \times 10} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
p                       &= \pounds 400
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Value $= \pounds 400$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                 &:& \text{\textdollar} \\\\
= & 1                       &:& 2.50 \\\\
= & 1 \times 400            &:& 2.50 \times 400 \\\\
= & \pounds 400             &:& \text{\textdollar} 1000
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Value $= \pounds 400$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2:1:30</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

$4$ oranges and $7$ apples weigh $51 \ \text{ounces}$.
$7$ oranges and $4$ apples weigh $48 \ \text{ounces}$.

What do $1$ apple and $1$ orange weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of $1$ apple $= a \ \text{ounces}$

Let the weight of $1$ orange $= b \ \text{ounces}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4b + 7a           &= 51 \\\\
7b + 4a           &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
4b + 7a + 7b + 4a           &= 51 + 48 \\\\
7a + 4a + 4b + 7b           &= 99 \\\\
11a + 11b                   &= 99 \\\\
11 \times ( a + b )         &= 99 \\\\
a + b                       &= \dfrac {99} {11} \\\\
a + b                       &= \dfrac {11 \times 9} {11} \\\\
a + b                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {11} \times 9} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a + b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

Weight of $1$ apple and $1$ orange $= 9 \ \text{ounces}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9 \ \text{ounces}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of $1$ apple $= a \ \text{ounces}$
<emptyline>
Let the weight of $1$ orange $= b \ \text{ounces}$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4b + 7a           &= 51 \\\\
7b + 4a           &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's add both the equations.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4b + 7a + 7b + 4a           &= 51 + 48 \\\\
7a + 4a + 4b + 7b           &= 99 \\\\
11a + 11b                   &= 99 \\\\
11 \times ( a + b )         &= 99 \\\\
a + b                       &= \dfrac {99} {11} \\\\
a + b                       &= \dfrac {11 \times 9} {11} \\\\
a + b                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {11} \times 9} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a + b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of $1$ apple and $1$ orange $= 9 \ \text{ounces}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9 \ \text{ounces}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g2 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>AD6895B3-4E61-4AAF-9E1F-1987110B5F92</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>halifax-grammar-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

The pie chart shows the proportions of different drinks chosen from a machine in one day.
There were $15$ cartons of pineapple juice taken. How many cartons of orange juice were taken? 

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


- $33$
- $35$
- $45$
- $120$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of cartons of orange juice $= a$

As per the pie chart, 

$
\begin{aligned}
\% \ \text{of oranges}      &= 100 - 13 - 11 - 28 - 15 \\\\
                            &= 33
\end{aligned}
$


Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
11 \ \% : 15 \ \text{cartons}                   &= 33 \ \% : a \ \text{cartons} \\\\
\dfrac {11} {15}                                &= \dfrac {33} {a} \\\\
11 \times a                                     &= 33 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {33 \times 15} {11} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {11 \times 3 \times 15} {11} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {11} \times 3 \times 15} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 45
\end{aligned}
$

Number of cartons of oranges $= 45$

</div>
<div class='working'>

As per the pie chart, 

$
\begin{aligned}
\% \ \text{of oranges}      &= 100 - 13 - 11 - 28 - 15 \\\\
                            &= 33
\end{aligned}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \%                         &:& \text{cartons} \\\\
= & 11                         &:& 15 \\\\
= & 1                          &:& \dfrac {15} {11} \\\\
= & 1 \times 33                &:& \dfrac {15 \times 33} {11} \\\\
= & 33                         &:& \dfrac {15 \times 3 \times 11} {11} \\\\
= & 33                         &:& \dfrac {15 \times 3 \times \cancel {11}} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
= & 33                         &:& 15 \times 3 \\\\
= & 33                         &:& 45
\end{array}
$

Number of cartons of oranges $= 45$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$45$

</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of cartons of orange juice $= a$

$

                            &= 33
\end{aligned}

a                                               &= \dfrac {33 \times 15} {11} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {11 \times 3 \times 15} {11} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {11} \times 3 \times 15} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 45
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Tim is going paintballing. Entry into paintballing costs $\pounds 23$, but then you also have to pay $\pounds 6$ per $100$ paintballs that you use.

Tim spent $\pounds 65$ in total. How many paintballs did he use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of using paintballs}       &= 65 - 23 \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 42
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of paintballs}     &= \dfrac {42} {6} \times 100 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {42 \times 100} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 7 \times 100} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 7 \times 100} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                &= 7 \times 100 \\\\
                                &= 700
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$700$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of using paintballs}       &= 65 - 23 \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 42
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of paintballs}     &= \dfrac {42} {6} \times 100 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {42 \times 100} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 7 \times 100} {6} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 7 \times 100} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                &= 7 \times 100 \\\\
                                &= 700
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$700$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Janice is running a bath. She  mixes hot water with cold water to make sure the temperature is just right.

She uses $5$ times more hot water than cold water. Janice's bath holds $90 \ \text{litres}$ of water in total. 
How much hot water does she use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of hot water $= a \ \text{litres}$

Volume of cold water $= (90 - a) \ \text{litres}$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{litres} : 1 \ \text{litres}           &= a \ \text{litres} : (90 - a) \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {5} {1}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {90 - a} \\\\
5 \times (90 - a)                               &= 1 \times a \\\\
450 - 5a                                        &= a \\\\
450                                             &= a + 5a \\\\
a + 5a                                          &= 450 \\\\
6a                                              &= 450 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {450} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 75} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 75} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 75 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Hot water used $= 75 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{total water}                        &:& \text{hot water} \\\\
= & 5 + 1                                     &:& 5  \\\\
= & 6 \ \text{litres}                         &:& 5 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 6 \times 15                               &:& 5 \times 15 \\\\
= & 90 \ \text{litres}                        &:& 75 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Hot water used $= 75 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of hot water $= a \ \text{litres}$
<emptyline>
Volume of cold water $= (90 - a) \ \text{litres}$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{litres} : 1 \ \text{litres}           &= a \ \text{litres} : (90 - a) \ \text{litres} \\\\

a                                               &= \dfrac {450} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 75} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 75} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 75 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Hot water used $= 75 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{total water}                        &:& \text{hot water} \\\\
= & 5 + 1                                     &:& 5  \\\\
= & 6 \ \text{litres}                         &:& 5 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 6 \times 15                               &:& 5 \times 15 \\\\
= & 90 \ \text{litres}                        &:& 75 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Hot water used $= 75 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Bob the Builder is filling a hole with a mixture of gravel and sand.
$234 \ \text{kg}$ of sand are needed to make $400 \ \text{kg}$ of the mixture.

Bob makes $1000 \ \text{kg}$ of the mixture.

How much **gravel** does Bob need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{mixture}                    &:& \text{gravel} \\\\
= & 400 \ \text{kg}                   &:& (400 - 234) \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 400                               &:& 166 \\\\
= & 1                                 &:& \dfrac {166} {400} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000                     &:& \dfrac {166 \times 1000} {400} \\\\
= & 1000                              &:& \dfrac {2 \times 83 \times 200 \times 5} {2 \times 200} \\\\
= & 1000                              &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 83 \times \cancel {200} \times 5} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {200}} \\\\
= & 1000                              &:& 83 \times 5 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{kg}                  &:& 415 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

Gravel needed $= 415 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$415 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$

= & 400 \ \text{kg}                   &:& (400 - 234) \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 400                               &:& 166 \\\\
= & 1                                 &:& \dfrac {166} {400} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000                     &:& \dfrac {166 \times 1000} {400} \\\\
= & 1000                              &:& \dfrac {2 \times 83 \times 200 \times 5} {2 \times 200} \\\\
= & 1000                              &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 83 \times \cancel {200} \times 5} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {200}} \\\\
= & 1000                              &:& 83 \times 5 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{kg}                  &:& 415 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Gravel needed $= 415 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Victoria the vet is weighing her pets.

Her rabbit and her cat together weigh $10 \ \text{kg}$.

Her cat and dog together weigh $24 \ \text{kg}$.

Her dog and rabbit together weigh $20 \ \text{kg}$.

How much do all $3$ of her pets weigh when weighed together?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of rabbit $= r$

Let the weight of cat $= c$

Let the weight of dog $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
r + c       &= 10 \\\\
c + d       &= 24 \\\\
d + r       &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add all the three equations together.

$
\begin{aligned}
r + c + c + d + d + r       &= 10 + 24 + 20 \\\\
r + r + c + c + d + d       &= 54 \\\\
2r + 2 c + 2d               &= 54 \\\\
2 \times (r + c + d)        &= 54 \\\\
r + c + d                   &= \dfrac {54} {2} \\\\
r + c + d                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 27} {2} \\\\
r + c + d                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 27} {\cancel 2} \\\\
r + c + d                   &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

Weight of all the $3$ pets together $= 27 \ \text{kg}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27 \ text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of rabbit $= r$
<emptyline>
Let the weight of cat $= c$
<emptyline>
Let the weight of dog $= d$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

c + d       &= 24 \\\\
d + r       &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

2 \times (r + c + d)        &= 54 \\\\
r + c + d                   &= \dfrac {54} {2} \\\\
r + c + d                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 27} {2} \\\\
r + c + d                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 27} {\cancel 2} \\\\
r + c + d                   &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of all the $3$ pets together $= 27 \ \text{kg}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
